# Research Group subset-b-000593

This grouped report covers Linux Devicetree network binding YAML schemas for Bluetooth controllers, Ethernet MAC/MDIO hardware, CAN controllers/transceivers, and DSA switches under the Ceph client source tree. Each section preserves the source path and is delimited for deterministic splitting into source-tree-aligned per-file research documents.

<!-- BEGIN_FILE_RESEARCH: sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/bluetooth/brcm,bluetooth.yaml -->
# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/bluetooth/brcm,bluetooth.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/bluetooth/brcm,bluetooth.yaml` defines the Bluetooth controller binding titled `Broadcom Bluetooth Chips`. This binding describes Broadcom UART-attached bluetooth chips. It is a Linux Devicetree YAML schema used to validate DTS/DTB nodes and to document the firmware ABI consumed before the kernel creates the corresponding Bluetooth HCI controller.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions
The public interface is the YAML/dt-schema ABI, not C-callable functions. `compatible` uses `oneOf` with 2 branches with 14 tokens: `infineon,cyw43439-bt`, `brcm,bcm4329-bt`, `brcm,bcm20702a1`, `brcm,bcm4330-bt`, `brcm,bcm4334-bt`, `brcm,bcm43430a0-bt`, `brcm,bcm43430a1-bt`, `brcm,bcm43438-bt`, `brcm,bcm4345c5`, `brcm,bcm43540-bt`, `brcm,bcm4335a0`, `brcm,bcm4349-bt`, `cypress,cyw4373a0-bt`, `infineon,cyw55572-bt`. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `shutdown-gpios`, `reset-gpios`, `device-wakeup-gpios`, `host-wakeup-gpios`, `clocks`, `clock-names`, `vbat-supply`, `vddio-supply`, `brcm,bt-pcm-int-params`, `brcm,requires-autobaud-mode`, `interrupts`, `interrupt-names`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`; required keys found in nested schemas include `compatible`. Pattern properties are none. Collected numeric/item constraints include `maxItems=1`, `minItems=1`, `maxItems=2`, `minItems=5`, `maxItems=5`. The highest-risk API details are compatible strings, wake/shutdown GPIO polarity, required power rails, clock names, transport-specific properties, and whether common Qualcomm/Broadcom/Realtek fragments stay aligned with chip-specific bindings. Important property roles: `compatible` selects the OF match table and stable hardware ABI; `shutdown-gpios` controls reset, shutdown, enable, or wake sequencing; `reset-gpios` controls reset, shutdown, enable, or wake sequencing; `device-wakeup-gpios` controls reset, shutdown, enable, or wake sequencing; `host-wakeup-gpios` controls reset, shutdown, enable, or wake sequencing; `clocks` connects clock providers and names; `clock-names` connects clock providers and names; `vbat-supply` links a regulator supply; `vddio-supply` links a regulator supply; `interrupts` routes controller, wake, link, or error IRQs; `interrupt-names` routes controller, wake, link, or error IRQs.

## Control Flow
Control flow is declarative schema evaluation. During `make dt_binding_check` or `make dtbs_check`, dt-schema loads the YAML, resolves `$ref` entries, matches by `$id`, `$nodename`, `compatible`, or fragment inclusion, checks required properties, evaluates `allOf`, `dependencies`, validates examples, and enforces `additionalProperties` or `unevaluatedProperties`. At runtime the YAML itself does not execute. Firmware provides a DTB node for the Bluetooth controller binding; Linux driver core or bus code matches a driver, then subsystem helpers consume the validated resources. Child schemas such as `ports`, `ethernet-ports`, MDIO children, or CAN transceiver links guide later parsing by DSA, phylink, PHYLIB, Bluetooth, or SocketCAN drivers.

## State and Persistence Behavior
The schema stores no mutable kernel state and writes no persistent data. Persistence is the Devicetree ABI: compatible strings, property names, array order, phandle cell counts, child-node names, and example layouts are contracts carried by DTS sources and deployed DTBs. Runtime state is owned by drivers after probe, including HCI registration, firmware download, controller power state, wake signaling, runtime PM, and Bluetooth stack state; this file only constrains how hardware and board wiring are represented.

## Dependencies and Integration Points
Maintainers listed: Linus Walleij <linusw@kernel.org>. Direct schema dependencies are `/schemas/serial/serial-peripheral-props.yaml#`, `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/uint8-array`. Integration points include Bluetooth HCI, serdev/UART or SDIO probing, regulator and clock providers, GPIO wake/reset lines, board DTS nodes, and the Linux Bluetooth stack. The binding participates in schema example extraction, Linux OF matching, and board DTS validation. Compatible scan found driver-side files: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/bluetooth/hci_bcm.c`. In-tree DTS users found by compatible scan: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/allwinner/sun7i-a20-cubietruck.dts`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/allwinner/sun8i-a33-vstar.dts`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/allwinner/sun8i-a83t-bananapi-m3.dts`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/allwinner/sun8i-a83t-cubietruck-plus.dts`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/allwinner/sun8i-a83t-tbs-a711.dts`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/allwinner/sun8i-h2-plus-bananapi-m2-zero.dts`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/allwinner/sun8i-h3-nanopi-duo2.dts`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/allwinner/sun8i-h3-nanopi-neo-air.dts`, and 2 more. The file contains 1 embedded example.

## Risks
Primary risks are incompatible ABI changes to compatible strings, wake/shutdown GPIO polarity, required power rails, clock names, transport-specific properties, and whether common Qualcomm/Broadcom/Realtek fragments stay aligned with chip-specific bindings, mismatches between documented compatibles and driver OF match tables, resource-order changes that pass schema review but break probe, and stale examples. This schema closes composed schemas with `unevaluatedProperties: false`. Additional risk signals: conditional branches can validate one silicon variant while rejecting another; large compatible lists are prone to missing or misordered fallback entries; GPIO polarity or naming mistakes can invert reset, shutdown, wake, or interrupt behavior.

## Test Signals
Run `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/bluetooth/brcm,bluetooth.yaml` for targeted schema validation and `make dtbs_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/bluetooth/brcm,bluetooth.yaml` against affected board DTS files. The schema has 1 example block, so example compilation is part of the signal. Review every compatible against driver `of_match_table` entries and exercise negative schema cases for missing required resources, bad child names, wrong `reg` values, and misspelled vendor properties. Runtime signals are HCI device registration, firmware load where required, suspend/resume wake tests, and GPIO/regulator sequencing.
<!-- END_FILE_RESEARCH: sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/bluetooth/brcm,bluetooth.yaml -->

Control flow is declarative schema evaluation. During `make dt_binding_check` or `make dtbs_check`, dt-schema loads the YAML, resolves `$ref` entries, matches by `$id`, `$nodename`, `compatible`, or fragment inclusion, checks required properties, evaluates `allOf`, validates examples, and enforces `additionalProperties` or `unevaluatedProperties`. At runtime the YAML itself does not execute. Firmware provides a DTB node for the Bluetooth controller binding; Linux driver core or bus code matches a driver, then subsystem helpers consume the validated resources. Child schemas such as `ports`, `ethernet-ports`, MDIO children, or CAN transceiver links guide later parsing by DSA, phylink, PHYLIB, Bluetooth, or SocketCAN drivers.

## State and Persistence Behavior
The schema stores no mutable kernel state and writes no persistent data. Persistence is the Devicetree ABI: compatible strings, property names, array order, phandle cell counts, child-node names, and example layouts are contracts carried by DTS sources and deployed DTBs. Runtime state is owned by drivers after probe, including HCI registration, firmware download, controller power state, wake signaling, runtime PM, and Bluetooth stack state; this file only constrains how hardware and board wiring are represented.

## Dependencies and Integration Points
Maintainers listed: Neeraj Sanjay Kale <neeraj.sanjaykale@nxp.com>. Direct schema dependencies are `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/uint32`, `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/uint8`, `bluetooth-controller.yaml#`. Integration points include Bluetooth HCI, serdev/UART or SDIO probing, regulator and clock providers, GPIO wake/reset lines, board DTS nodes, and the Linux Bluetooth stack. The binding participates in schema example extraction, Linux OF matching, and board DTS validation. Compatible scan found driver-side files: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/bluetooth/btnxpuart.c`. In-tree DTS users found by compatible scan: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/nxp/imx/imx6sll-kobo-clara2e-common.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/freescale/imx8dxl-evk.dts`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/freescale/imx8mm-evk.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/freescale/imx8mm-var-som-wifi-bt-iw61x.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/freescale/imx8mn-evk.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/freescale/imx8mp-beacon-som.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/freescale/imx8mp-evk.dts`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/freescale/imx8mp-tqma8mpql-mba8mp-ras314.dts`, and 2 more. The file contains 1 embedded example.

## Risks
Primary risks are incompatible ABI changes to compatible strings, wake/shutdown GPIO polarity, required power rails, clock names, transport-specific properties, and whether common Qualcomm/Broadcom/Realtek fragments stay aligned with chip-specific bindings, mismatches between documented compatibles and driver OF match tables, resource-order changes that pass schema review but break probe, and stale examples. This schema closes composed schemas with `unevaluatedProperties: false`. Additional risk signals: conditional branches can validate one silicon variant while rejecting another; GPIO polarity or naming mistakes can invert reset, shutdown, wake, or interrupt behavior.

## Test Signals
Run `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/bluetooth/nxp,88w8987-bt.yaml` for targeted schema validation and `make dtbs_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/bluetooth/nxp,88w8987-bt.yaml` against affected board DTS files. The schema has 1 example block, so example compilation is part of the signal. Review every compatible against driver `of_match_table` entries and exercise negative schema cases for missing required resources, bad child names, wrong `reg` values, and misspelled vendor properties. Runtime signals are HCI device registration, firmware load where required, suspend/resume wake tests, and GPIO/regulator sequencing.

Primary risks are incompatible ABI changes to compatible strings, wake/shutdown GPIO polarity, required power rails, clock names, transport-specific properties, and whether common Qualcomm/Broadcom/Realtek fragments stay aligned with chip-specific bindings, mismatches between documented compatibles and driver OF match tables, resource-order changes that pass schema review but break probe, and stale examples. # sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/bluetooth/qcom,wcn3990-bt.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/bluetooth/qcom,wcn3990-bt.yaml` defines the Bluetooth controller binding titled `Qualcomm WCN3990/WCN3991/WCN3998 Bluetooth`. It constrains compatible strings, required resources, common schema references, and optional child nodes before Linux network, Bluetooth, CAN, MDIO, Ethernet, or DSA drivers consume the node at probe time.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions
The public interface is the YAML/dt-schema ABI, not C-callable functions. `compatible` uses an `enum` with 3 tokens: `qcom,wcn3990-bt`, `qcom,wcn3991-bt`, `qcom,wcn3998-bt`. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `clocks`, `vddch0-supply`, `vddch1-supply`, `vddio-supply`, `vddrf-supply`, `vddxo-supply`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `vddch0-supply`, `vddio-supply`, `vddrf-supply`, `vddxo-supply`; required keys found in nested schemas include `compatible`, `vddch0-supply`, `vddio-supply`, `vddrf-supply`, `vddxo-supply`. Pattern properties are none. Collected numeric/item constraints include no explicit min/max/item constraints collected. The highest-risk API details are compatible strings, wake/shutdown GPIO polarity, required power rails, clock names, transport-specific properties, and whether common Qualcomm/Broadcom/Realtek fragments stay aligned with chip-specific bindings. Important property roles: `compatible` selects the OF match table and stable hardware ABI; `clocks` connects clock providers and names; `vddch0-supply` links a regulator supply; `vddch1-supply` links a regulator supply; `vddio-supply` links a regulator supply; `vddrf-supply` links a regulator supply; `vddxo-supply` links a regulator supply.

## Control Flow
Control flow is declarative schema evaluation. During `make dt_binding_check` or `make dtbs_check`, dt-schema loads the YAML, resolves `$ref` entries, matches by `$id`, `$nodename`, `compatible`, or fragment inclusion, checks required properties, evaluates `allOf`, validates examples, and enforces `additionalProperties` or `unevaluatedProperties`. At runtime the YAML itself does not execute. Firmware provides a DTB node for the Bluetooth controller binding; Linux driver core or bus code matches a driver, then subsystem helpers consume the validated resources. Child schemas such as `ports`, `ethernet-ports`, MDIO children, or CAN transceiver links guide later parsing by DSA, phylink, PHYLIB, Bluetooth, or SocketCAN drivers.

## State and Persistence Behavior
The schema stores no mutable kernel state and writes no persistent data. Persistence is the Devicetree ABI: compatible strings, property names, array order, phandle cell counts, child-node names, and example layouts are contracts carried by DTS sources and deployed DTBs. Runtime state is owned by drivers after probe, including HCI registration, firmware download, controller power state, wake signaling, runtime PM, and Bluetooth stack state; this file only constrains how hardware and board wiring are represented.

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/bluetooth/realtek,bluetooth.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/bluetooth/realtek,bluetooth.yaml` defines the Bluetooth controller binding titled `RTL8723BS/RTL8723CS/RTL8821CS/RTL8822CS Bluetooth`. RTL8723BS/RTL8723CS/RTL8821CS/RTL8822CS is a WiFi + BT chip. It is a Linux Devicetree YAML schema used to validate DTS/DTB nodes and to document the firmware ABI consumed before the kernel creates the corresponding Bluetooth HCI controller.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions
The public interface is the YAML/dt-schema ABI, not C-callable functions. `compatible` uses `oneOf` with 2 branches with 5 tokens: `realtek,rtl8723bs-bt`, `realtek,rtl8723cs-bt`, `realtek,rtl8723ds-bt`, `realtek,rtl8822cs-bt`, `realtek,rtl8821cs-bt`. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `device-wake-gpios`, `enable-gpios`, `host-wake-gpios`, `max-speed`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`; required keys found in nested schemas include `compatible`. Pattern properties are none. Collected numeric/item constraints include `maxItems=1`. The highest-risk API details are compatible strings, wake/shutdown GPIO polarity, required power rails, clock names, transport-specific properties, and whether common Qualcomm/Broadcom/Realtek fragments stay aligned with chip-specific bindings. Important property roles: `compatible` selects the OF match table and stable hardware ABI; `device-wake-gpios` describes board GPIO wiring; `enable-gpios` controls reset, shutdown, enable, or wake sequencing; `host-wake-gpios` describes board GPIO wiring.

## Control Flow
Control flow is declarative schema evaluation. During `make dt_binding_check` or `make dtbs_check`, dt-schema loads the YAML, resolves `$ref` entries, matches by `$id`, `$nodename`, `compatible`, or fragment inclusion, checks required properties, evaluates `allOf`, validates examples, and enforces `additionalProperties` or `unevaluatedProperties`. At runtime the YAML itself does not execute. Firmware provides a DTB node for the Bluetooth controller binding; Linux driver core or bus code matches a driver, then subsystem helpers consume the validated resources. Child schemas such as `ports`, `ethernet-ports`, MDIO children, or CAN transceiver links guide later parsing by DSA, phylink, PHYLIB, Bluetooth, or SocketCAN drivers.

## State and Persistence Behavior
The schema stores no mutable kernel state and writes no persistent data. Persistence is the Devicetree ABI: compatible strings, property names, array order, phandle cell counts, child-node names, and example layouts are contracts carried by DTS sources and deployed DTBs. Runtime state is owned by drivers after probe, including HCI registration, firmware download, controller power state, wake signaling, runtime PM, and Bluetooth stack state; this file only constrains how hardware and board wiring are represented.

## Dependencies and Integration Points
Maintainers listed: Vasily Khoruzhick <anarsoul@gmail.com>, Alistair Francis <alistair@alistair23.me>. Direct schema dependencies are `/schemas/serial/serial-peripheral-props.yaml#`. ## Control Flow

During `make dt_binding_check` or `make dtbs_check`, dt-schema loads the YAML, resolves `$ref` entries, matches by `$id`, `$nodename`, `compatible`, or fragment inclusion, checks required properties, evaluates `allOf`, validates examples, and enforces `additionalProperties` or `unevaluatedProperties`. At runtime the YAML itself does not execute. Firmware provides a DTB node for the CAN controller binding; Linux driver core or bus code matches a driver, then subsystem helpers consume the validated resources. Child schemas such as `ports`, `ethernet-ports`, MDIO children, or CAN transceiver links guide later parsing by DSA, phylink, PHYLIB, Bluetooth, or SocketCAN drivers.

## State and Persistence Behavior
The schema stores no mutable kernel state and writes no persistent data. Persistence is the Devicetree ABI: compatible strings, property names, array order, phandle cell counts, child-node names, and example layouts are contracts carried by DTS sources and deployed DTBs. Runtime state is owned by drivers after probe, including netdev registration, bit-timing programming, bus-off/error counters, transceiver enablement, RX/TX mailboxes, and SocketCAN state; this file only constrains how hardware and board wiring are represented.

## Dependencies and Integration Points
Maintainers listed: Chandrasekar Ramakrishnan <rcsekar@samsung.com>. Direct schema dependencies are `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/int32-array`, `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/phandle-array`, `can-controller.yaml#`, `can-transceiver.yaml#`. Integration points include the Linux CAN and SocketCAN stack, platform/SPI controllers, clocks, resets, interrupt controllers, transceiver phandles, regulator supplies, pinctrl, and board DTS CAN nodes. The binding participates in schema example extraction, Linux OF matching, and board DTS validation. Compatible scan found driver-side files: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/net/can/m_can/m_can_platform.c`. In-tree DTS users found by compatible scan: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/microchip/lan966x.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/microchip/sam9x7.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/microchip/sama5d2.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/microchip/sama7d65.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/microchip/sama7g5.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/st/stm32mp133.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/st/stm32mp153.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/ti/omap/dra76x.dtsi`, and 2 more. The file contains 2 embedded examples.

## Risks
Primary risks are incompatible ABI changes to clock and interrupt ordering, `can-transceiver` links, bit-rate capability properties, controller-specific compatible fallbacks, register windows, and optional timestamp/DMA resources, mismatches between documented compatibles and driver OF match tables, resource-order changes that pass schema review but break probe, and stale examples. This schema closes composed schemas with `unevaluatedProperties: false`. Additional risk signals: conditional branches can validate one silicon variant while rejecting another; CAN timing and transceiver capability units must match driver expectations.

## Test Signals
Run `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/can/bosch,m_can.yaml` for targeted schema validation and `make dtbs_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/can/bosch,m_can.yaml` against affected board DTS files. The schema has 2 example blocks, so example compilation is part of the signal. Review every compatible against driver `of_match_table` entries and exercise negative schema cases for missing required resources, bad child names, wrong `reg` values, and misspelled vendor properties. Runtime signals are CAN interface registration, loopback or bus traffic tests, bus-off recovery, interrupt delivery, and transceiver enable tests.

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/can/microchip,mcp251xfd.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/can/microchip,mcp251xfd.yaml` defines the CAN controller binding titled `Microchip MCP2517FD, MCP2518FD and MCP251863 stand-alone CAN controller`. It constrains compatible strings, required resources, common schema references, and optional child nodes before Linux network, Bluetooth, CAN, MDIO, Ethernet, or DSA drivers consume the node at probe time.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions
The public interface is the YAML/dt-schema ABI, not C-callable functions. `compatible` uses `oneOf` with 2 branches with 4 tokens: `microchip,mcp2517fd`, `microchip,mcp2518fd`, `microchip,mcp251xfd`, `microchip,mcp251863`. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `interrupts`, `clocks`, `vdd-supply`, `xceiver-supply`, `microchip,rx-int-gpios`, `microchip,xstbyen`, `spi-max-frequency`, `gpio-controller`, `#gpio-cells`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `interrupts`, `clocks`; required keys found in nested schemas include `compatible`, `reg`, `interrupts`, `clocks`. Pattern properties are none. Collected numeric/item constraints include `maxItems=1`, `maximum=20000000`. The highest-risk API details are clock and interrupt ordering, `can-transceiver` links, bit-rate capability properties, controller-specific compatible fallbacks, register windows, and optional timestamp/DMA resources. Important property roles: `compatible` selects the OF match table and stable hardware ABI; `reg` carries MMIO ranges, bus addresses, chip selects, or switch port indexes; `interrupts` routes controller, wake, link, or error IRQs; `clocks` connects clock providers and names; `vdd-supply` links a regulator supply; `xceiver-supply` links a regulator supply; `microchip,rx-int-gpios` describes board GPIO wiring; `#gpio-cells` fixes child-node or provider cell layout.

## Control Flow
Control flow is declarative schema evaluation. During `make dt_binding_check` or `make dtbs_check`, dt-schema loads the YAML, resolves `$ref` entries, matches by `$id`, `$nodename`, `compatible`, or fragment inclusion, checks required properties, evaluates `allOf`, validates examples, and enforces `additionalProperties` or `unevaluatedProperties`. At runtime the YAML itself does not execute. Firmware provides a DTB node for the CAN controller binding; Linux driver core or bus code matches a driver, then subsystem helpers consume the validated resources. Child schemas such as `ports`, `ethernet-ports`, MDIO children, or CAN transceiver links guide later parsing by DSA, phylink, PHYLIB, Bluetooth, or SocketCAN drivers.

## State and Persistence Behavior
The schema stores no mutable kernel state and writes no persistent data. Persistence is the Devicetree ABI: compatible strings, property names, array order, phandle cell counts, child-node names, and example layouts are contracts carried by DTS sources and deployed DTBs. Runtime state is owned by drivers after probe, including netdev registration, bit-timing programming, bus-off/error counters, transceiver enablement, RX/TX mailboxes, and SocketCAN state; this file only constrains how hardware and board wiring are represented.

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/cdns,macb.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/cdns,macb.yaml` defines the Ethernet MAC/controller binding titled `Cadence MACB/GEM Ethernet controller`. It constrains compatible strings, required resources, common schema references, and optional child nodes before Linux network, Bluetooth, CAN, MDIO, Ethernet, or DSA drivers consume the node at probe time.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions
The public interface is the YAML/dt-schema ABI, not C-callable functions. `compatible` uses `oneOf` with 10 branches with 28 tokens: `cdns,at91rm9200-emac`, `cdns,emac`, `cdns,zynq-gem`, `cdns,zynqmp-gem`, `cdns,gem`, `xlnx,versal-gem`, `xlnx,zynq-gem`, `xlnx,zynqmp-gem`, `cdns,at91sam9260-macb`, `cdns,sam9x60-macb`, `microchip,mpfs-macb`, `cdns,macb`, `microchip,pic64gx-macb`, `atmel,sama5d3-macb`, `atmel,sama5d2-gem`, `atmel,sama5d29-gem`, `atmel,sama5d3-gem`, `atmel,sama5d4-gem`, and 10 more. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `interrupts`, `clocks`, `clock-names`, `local-mac-address`, `phy-mode`, `phy-handle`, `phys`, `resets`, `reset-names`, `fixed-link`, `iommus`, `power-domains`, `cdns,refclk-ext`, `cdns,refclk-source`, `cdns,rx-watermark`, `cdns,timer-adjust`, `#address-cells`, `#size-cells`, `mdio`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `interrupts`, `clocks`, `clock-names`, `phy-mode`; required keys found in nested schemas include `compatible`, `reg`, `interrupts`, `clocks`, `clock-names`, `phy-mode`, `phys`. Pattern properties are `^ethernet-phy@[0-9a-f]$`. Collected numeric/item constraints include `minItems=1`, `maxItems=8`, `maxItems=5`, `maxItems=1`. The highest-risk API details are register and interrupt ordering, `phy-mode`, PHY/fixed-link selection, DMA/clock/reset names, MAC address sources, compatible fallback ordering, and optional queue or port child nodes. Important property roles: `compatible` selects the OF match table and stable hardware ABI; `reg` carries MMIO ranges, bus addresses, chip selects, or switch port indexes; `interrupts` routes controller, wake, link, or error IRQs; `clocks` connects clock providers and names; `clock-names` connects clock providers and names; `local-mac-address` provides a MAC address source; `phy-mode` binds MAC/switch ports to PHY or fixed-link mode; `phy-handle` binds MAC/switch ports to PHY or fixed-link mode; `phys` binds MAC/switch ports to PHY or fixed-link mode; `resets` controls reset, shutdown, enable, or wake sequencing; `reset-names` controls reset, shutdown, enable, or wake sequencing; `fixed-link` binds MAC/switch ports to PHY or fixed-link mode; `#address-cells` fixes child-node or provider cell layout; `#size-cells` fixes child-node or provider cell layout.

## Control Flow
Control flow is declarative schema evaluation. During `make dt_binding_check` or `make dtbs_check`, dt-schema loads the YAML, resolves `$ref` entries, matches by `$id`, `$nodename`, `compatible`, or fragment inclusion, checks required properties, evaluates `allOf`, validates examples, and enforces `additionalProperties` or `unevaluatedProperties`. At runtime the YAML itself does not execute. Firmware provides a DTB node for the Ethernet MAC/controller binding; Linux driver core or bus code matches a driver, then subsystem helpers consume the validated resources. Child schemas such as `ports`, `ethernet-ports`, MDIO children, or CAN transceiver links guide later parsing by DSA, phylink, PHYLIB, Bluetooth, or SocketCAN drivers.

## State and Persistence Behavior
The schema stores no mutable kernel state and writes no persistent data. Persistence is the Devicetree ABI: compatible strings, property names, array order, phandle cell counts, child-node names, and example layouts are contracts carried by DTS sources and deployed DTBs. Runtime state is owned by drivers after probe, including netdev registration, DMA descriptor ownership, PHY/phylink state, MAC address selection, link mode, NAPI polling, and queue statistics; this file only constrains how hardware and board wiring are represented.

## Dependencies and Integration Points
Maintainers listed: Nicolas Ferre <nicolas.ferre@microchip.com>, Claudiu Beznea <claudiu.beznea@microchip.com>. Direct schema dependencies are `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/string`, `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/uint32`, `ethernet-controller.yaml#`, `ethernet-phy.yaml#`. Integration points include the Linux netdev stack, platform/SPI bus probing, PHYLIB/phylink, MDIO, DMA, clocks, resets, interrupts, NVMEM MAC-address cells, fixed-link or external PHY nodes, and board DTS Ethernet topology. The binding participates in schema example extraction, Linux OF matching, and board DTS validation. Compatible scan found driver-side files: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/net/ethernet/cadence/macb_main.c`. In-tree DTS users found by compatible scan: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/microchip/at91rm9200.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/microchip/at91sam9260.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/microchip/at91sam9263.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/microchip/at91sam9g45.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/microchip/at91sam9x5_macb0.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/microchip/at91sam9x5_macb1.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/microchip/sam9x60.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/microchip/sam9x7.dtsi`, and 2 more. The file contains 2 embedded examples.

## Risks
Primary risks are incompatible ABI changes to register and interrupt ordering, `phy-mode`, PHY/fixed-link selection, DMA/clock/reset names, MAC address sources, compatible fallback ordering, and optional queue or port child nodes, mismatches between documented compatibles and driver OF match tables, resource-order changes that pass schema review but break probe, and stale examples. This schema closes composed schemas with `unevaluatedProperties: false`. Additional risk signals: conditional branches can validate one silicon variant while rejecting another; regex child schemas can over-match or under-match real nodes; large compatible lists are prone to missing or misordered fallback entries; port and PHY topology mistakes may compile but break link bring-up; GPIO polarity or naming mistakes can invert reset, shutdown, wake, or interrupt behavior.

## Test Signals
Run `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/cdns,macb.yaml` for targeted schema validation and `make dtbs_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/cdns,macb.yaml` against affected board DTS files. The schema has 2 example blocks, so example compilation is part of the signal. Review every compatible against driver `of_match_table` entries and exercise negative schema cases for missing required resources, bad child names, wrong `reg` values, and misspelled vendor properties. Runtime signals are netdev probe, MAC address selection, PHY/fixed-link negotiation, packet RX/TX, NAPI/IRQ behavior, and suspend/resume.
<!-- END_FILE_RESEARCH: sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/cdns,macb.yaml -->

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/dsa/arrow,xrs700x.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/dsa/arrow,xrs700x.yaml` defines the DSA Ethernet switch binding titled `Arrow SpeedChips XRS7000 Series Switch`. The Arrow SpeedChips XRS7000 Series of single chip gigabit Ethernet switches are designed for critical networking applications. # sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/dsa/micrel,ks8995.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/dsa/micrel,ks8995.yaml` defines the DSA Ethernet switch binding titled `Micrel KS8995 Family DSA Switches`. The Micrel KS8995 DSA Switches are 100 Mbit switches that were produced in the early-to-mid 2000s. It is a Linux Devicetree YAML schema used to validate DTS/DTB nodes and to document the firmware ABI consumed before the kernel creates the corresponding distributed switch architecture node.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions
The public interface is the YAML/dt-schema ABI, not C-callable functions. `compatible` uses an `enum` with 3 tokens: `micrel,ks8995`, `micrel,ksz8795`, `micrel,ksz8864`. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `reset-gpios`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `reg`; required keys found in nested schemas include `compatible`, `reg`. Pattern properties are none. Collected numeric/item constraints include `maxItems=1`. The highest-risk API details are port numbering, CPU/DSA/user port roles, fixed-link and PHY phandles, MDIO child buses, compatible fallback ordering, interrupt/reset lines, and any vendor LED or management subnodes. Important property roles: `compatible` selects the OF match table and stable hardware ABI; `reg` carries MMIO ranges, bus addresses, chip selects, or switch port indexes; `reset-gpios` controls reset, shutdown, enable, or wake sequencing.

## Control Flow
Control flow is declarative schema evaluation. During `make dt_binding_check` or `make dtbs_check`, dt-schema loads the YAML, resolves `$ref` entries, matches by `$id`, `$nodename`, `compatible`, or fragment inclusion, checks required properties, evaluates `allOf`, validates examples, and enforces `additionalProperties` or `unevaluatedProperties`. At runtime the YAML itself does not execute. Firmware provides a DTB node for the DSA Ethernet switch binding; Linux driver core or bus code matches a driver, then subsystem helpers consume the validated resources. Child schemas such as `ports`, `ethernet-ports`, MDIO children, or CAN transceiver links guide later parsing by DSA, phylink, PHYLIB, Bluetooth, or SocketCAN drivers.

## State and Persistence Behavior
The schema stores no mutable kernel state and writes no persistent data. Persistence is the Devicetree ABI: compatible strings, property names, array order, phandle cell counts, child-node names, and example layouts are contracts carried by DTS sources and deployed DTBs. Runtime state is owned by drivers after probe, including switch tree registration, port bridge/VLAN/FDB state, phylink state, MDIO transactions, CPU-port tagging, and hardware forwarding tables; this file only constrains how hardware and board wiring are represented.

## Dependencies and Integration Points
Maintainers listed: Linus Walleij <linusw@kernel.org>. Direct schema dependencies are `/schemas/spi/spi-peripheral-props.yaml#`, `dsa.yaml#/$defs/ethernet-ports`. Integration points include the DSA core, switchdev, phylink/PHYLIB, MDIO/SPI/I2C parent buses, `ethernet-ports` or `ports` child nodes, CPU/user port topology, bridge/VLAN offload, and board DTS switch nodes. The binding participates in schema example extraction, Linux OF matching, and board DTS validation. In-tree DTS users found by compatible scan: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/intel/ixp/intel-ixp42x-actiontec-mi424wr.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/intel/ixp/intel-ixp42x-linksys-wrv54g.dts`. The file contains 1 embedded example.

## Risks
Primary risks are incompatible ABI changes to port numbering, CPU/DSA/user port roles, fixed-link and PHY phandles, MDIO child buses, compatible fallback ordering, interrupt/reset lines, and any vendor LED or management subnodes, mismatches between documented compatibles and driver OF match tables, resource-order changes that pass schema review but break probe, and stale examples. This schema closes composed schemas with `unevaluatedProperties: false`. Additional risk signals: conditional branches can validate one silicon variant while rejecting another; GPIO polarity or naming mistakes can invert reset, shutdown, wake, or interrupt behavior.

The Motorcomm YT921x series is a family of Ethernet switches with up to 8 internal GbE PHYs and up to 2 GMACs, including: - YT9215S / YT9215RB / YT9215SC: 5 GbE PHYs (Port 0-4) + 2 GMACs (Port 8-9) - YT9213NB: 2 GbE PHYs (Port 1/3) + 1 GMAC (Port 9) - YT9214NB: 2 GbE PHYs (Port 1/3) + 2 GMACs (Port 8-9) - YT9218N: 8 GbE PHYs (Port 0-7) - YT9218MB: 8 GbE PHYs (Port 0-7) + 2 GMACs (Port 8-9) Any port can be used as the CPU port. `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/dsa/nxp,sja1105.yaml` defines the DSA Ethernet switch binding titled `NXP SJA1105 Automotive Ethernet Switch Family`. ## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/net/dsa/vitesse,vsc73xx.yaml` defines the DSA Ethernet switch binding titled `Vitesse VSC73xx DSA Switches`. The Vitesse DSA Switches were produced in the early-to-mid 2000s. It is a Linux Devicetree YAML schema used to validate DTS/DTB nodes and to document the firmware ABI consumed before the kernel creates the corresponding distributed switch architecture node.
